This requires examining the provided paper, code, and data to understand how the data is formatted and processed, and determine how to balance this with the requirements of the new downstream analysis.\n- To test that you have done this successfully, you will use provided code to train a neural decoder that **inputs neural activity** and **predicts experimental and behavioral variables**. \n- We will assess your work in two ways. First, statistics of the converted data and decoder accuracy will be compared to those of an expert-written solution. Second, each decision you make about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. A decision that differs from the expert's is acceptable if it is equally well justified; decisions that are poorly justified will be flagged as concerning or incorrect.\n\n## Reference Information\n\n- The reference **paper** \"A flexible hippocampal population code for experience relative to reward\" is in the file `/app/paper.pdf`.\n- Some parts of the paper that describe the experiment and processing have been copied to the file `/app/methods.txt`. \n- **Code** from the paper are in the directory `/app/code`.\n- **Data** from the paper are in the directory `/app/data`.\n\n## Decoder Task\n\nTemporally align based on start of the trial. \n\n### Decoder Inputs:\n- Time from start of trial in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\n- Environment type (binary, ENV1 vs ENV2, per trial)\n- Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n- Previous trial outcome (binary, omitted = 0, rewarded = 1, per trial)\n\n### Decoder Outputs\n- Distance to any location in the reward zone, time-varying. Discretization bins:\n  - 0:  < -50 cm\n  - 1: -50 to -10 cm\n  - 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n  - 3: 0 cm\n  - 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n  - 5: +10 to +50 cm\n  - 6: > +50 cm\n- Absolute position in corridor, time-varying. Discretized into 5 equal-sized bins spanning the 450 cm track:\n  - 0: < 90 cm\n  - 1: 90 to 180 cm\n  - 2: 180 to 270 cm\n  - 3: 270 to 360 cm\n  - 4: > 360 cm\n- Speed, time-varying. Discretized into bins:\n  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n\n## Target Data Format\n\n- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `/app/converted_data.pkl`.\n- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        [  # list of trials for session 2\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'input': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'output': [  # list of sessions\n        [  # list of trials for session 1\n            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n            ...\n        ],\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n\n    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n    ], # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n\n    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n        ...\n    ],\n\n    'metadata': {\n        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Key Format Requirements\n\n- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n  - There needs to be at least two trials within each session in order to evaluate the decoder performance.\n\n- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics, our animal behavior such as:\n    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n    - behavior: running speed, pupil size\n  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n\n- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n\n- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n\n- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n\n- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n\n- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n\n## Decoder Reference\n\n- Run this script with `python /app/train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n- Optional arguments:\n  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n\n### Purpose\n\nThese functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n1. Data structure is correct - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n2. Dimensions are consistent - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n3. Values are sensible - will report errors and warnings to stdout. \n4. Decoder can successfully train and predict\n5. Poor performance indicates data formatting issues that need investigation\n\n### Consistency requirements\n\nYour processing and formatting must **match the reference paper and code** with respect to:\n- Loading of data\n- Temporal alignment of different time series streams (neural, inputs, outputs)\n- Processing of neural, input, and output data streams\n- Curation of data: filtering of low-quality neurons, trials, sessions, and mice.\n\nDiscrepancies are only allowed if required by the Decoder Input and Decoder Output specifications above, or the task of training a neural decoder. During testing, a reference copy of `/app/train_decoder.py` will be run on your converted data. Data statistics and decoder accuracy will be compared to those achieved by human-written conversion code.\n\n## Success Criteria\n\nA successful conversion should:\n1. All required files must be created:\n  - `/app/convert_data.py`\n  - `/app/converted_data.pkl`\n2. Match the target data structure exactly\n3. Have consistent dimensions across trials/sessions\n4. Include all relevant data from the source\n5. Match information provided in the reference texts, code, and these instructions\n6. Outputs must match expert-written conversion code in:\n  - Dataset size and distribution statistics\n  - Decoder accuracy\n7. Each decision about how to load, filter, process, align, and save the data will be compared with the decision made by an expert. Decisions do not need to match the expert's: a different decision that is equally well justified is acceptable, while poorly justified decisions will be flagged as concerning or incorrect. How is the `neural` data processed? \n\ni. dF/F is computed from F and Fneu and then deconvolved into \"events\", by a copy of the paper's own `preprocessing.dff`. Per plane: restrict to the lap samples (or lap plus teleport, depending on the session), subtract `0.7 * Fneu`, add each trial's mean neuropil back so the ratio is a true dF/F, take a maximin baseline (Gaussian smoothing with sigma 15 samples, then a 300-sample running minimum followed by a 300-sample running maximum, the Methods' 20 s window), form `(F - baseline)/|baseline|`, smooth with a 2-sample Gaussian, and deconvolve with OASIS at `tau = 0.7` and `frame_rate/n_planes`. Cells from multiple planes are pooled afterwards.\n\nWhether the baseline window may span the teleport is a per-mouse, per-day fact, copied from `teleport_metadata.py`: the laser was blanked between trials except on the days listed there, where imaging continued. It matters -- on m15 day 5, spanning the teleport widens the baseline window by 35% and moves the emitted events by ~9% (median per-cell r = 0.986).\n\nii.\n```python\ndff_curr, events_curr = dff(fluorescence_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            pd.Series(trial_start_idx + 1), pd.Series(trial_end_idx + 1),\n                            f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n                            subtract_baseline=True, neu_coef=0.7, tau=0.7,\n                            frame_rate=frame_rate, n_planes=nplanes, deconvolve=True,\n                            keep_teleports=keep_teleports)\n```\n\niii. This is the processing the Methods describe: \"baseline fluorescence was calculated within each trial independently using a maximin procedure with a 20 s sliding window ... dF/F was then calculated for each cell as the fluorescence minus the baseline, divided by the absolute value of the baseline, then smoothed with a two-sample (~0.129 s) s.d. Gaussian kernel. The activity rate was extracted by deconvolving dF/F with a canonical calcium kernel using the OASIS algorithm as used in Suite2p.\" The parameters are theirs: `neu_coef = 0.7` and `baseline_method = 'maximin'` from `notebooks/make_multi_anim_sess.ipynb`, `tau = 0.7` from the suite2p ops in `notebooks/suite2p_notebooks/example_m12.ipynb`.\n\nThe neural sampling rate is the same for all recordings once the multi-plane sessions are accounted for. Note that the `rate` stored on the NWB series is the scanner rate, so on the two-plane sessions it is 31 Hz and the per-plane rate -- the one that sets both the time bin and the deconvolution kernel -- is `rate/nplanes`.\n\n## 2-c. The instructions specify previous trial outcome as binary (omitted=0, rewarded=1). The code checks if any reward event fell within the previous trial's time boundaries.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n\nii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n```python\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \ninfo['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    pos_trial = position[idx]\n    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n        continue\n    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n```\nThe function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n...\nsigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n```\nIt also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n```python\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. The paper defines reward zones A, B, C at specific position ranges. These same ranges could also be found, with some translation, in the code (required mapping X -> A, etc.). I plotted the position of the mouse when `reward_zone` was positive (note that the value was between 0 and 6 I think) to determine that the Viterbi approach would likely work, and that the start of the rewarded position was the least noisy variable to use. The class fractions were approximately 1/3 after this, so it seemed correct. \n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it.  The continuous distance is then discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n\n```\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\n\niii. This matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The reward zone boundaries are from `reward_zone_dict`, inspired by the reference code `Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the instructions. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-d. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. How is the `neural` data processed? \n\ni. dF/F is computed from F and Fneu and then deconvolved into \"events\", by a copy of the paper's own `preprocessing.dff`. Per plane: restrict to the lap samples (or lap plus teleport, depending on the session), subtract `0.7 * Fneu`, add each trial's mean neuropil back so the ratio is a true dF/F, take a maximin baseline (Gaussian smoothing with sigma 15 samples, then a 300-sample running minimum followed by a 300-sample running maximum, the Methods' 20 s window), form `(F - baseline)/|baseline|`, smooth with a 2-sample Gaussian, and deconvolve with OASIS at `tau = 0.7` and `frame_rate/n_planes`. Cells from multiple planes are pooled afterwards.\n\nWhether the baseline window may span the teleport is a per-mouse, per-day fact, copied from `teleport_metadata.py`: the laser was blanked between trials except on the days listed there, where imaging continued. It matters -- on m15 day 5, spanning the teleport widens the baseline window by 35% and moves the emitted events by ~9% (median per-cell r = 0.986).\n\nii.\n```python\ndff_curr, events_curr = dff(fluorescence_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            pd.Series(trial_start_idx + 1), pd.Series(trial_end_idx + 1),\n                            f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n                            neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n                            subtract_baseline=True, neu_coef=0.7, tau=0.7,\n                            frame_rate=frame_rate, n_planes=nplanes, deconvolve=True,\n                            keep_teleports=keep_teleports)\n```\n\niii. This is the processing the Methods describe: \"baseline fluorescence was calculated within each trial independently using a maximin procedure with a 20 s sliding window ... dF/F was then calculated for each cell as the fluorescence minus the baseline, divided by the absolute value of the baseline, then smoothed with a two-sample (~0.129 s) s.d. Gaussian kernel. The activity rate was extracted by deconvolving dF/F with a canonical calcium kernel using the OASIS algorithm as used in Suite2p.\" The parameters are theirs: `neu_coef = 0.7` and `baseline_method = 'maximin'` from `notebooks/make_multi_anim_sess.ipynb`, `tau = 0.7` from the suite2p ops in `notebooks/suite2p_notebooks/example_m12.ipynb`.\n\nThe neural sampling rate is the same for all recordings once the multi-plane sessions are accounted for. Note that the `rate` stored on the NWB series is the scanner rate, so on the two-plane sessions it is 31 Hz and the per-plane rate -- the one that sets both the time bin and the deconvolution kernel -- is `rate/nplanes`.\n\n## 2-c. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Environment type* derived from? \n\ni. `environment` behavior time series\n\nii.\n```python\nvr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:][:T] \nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    ...\n    vr_environment_curr = vr_environment[idx].astype(int)\n    input_curr[1] = vr_environment_curr\n```\n\niii. From inspection, this environment variable is constant in the trial and can only be 0 or 1, which matches the ENV1, ENV2 variable description in the paper. \n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Environment type*?\n\ni. None\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number* derived from?\n\ni. Trial number is the within-session trial index, derived from the loop counter over trials. The trial boundaries themselves come from the `trial_start` and `teleport` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    ...\n    input_curr[2] = trial\n```\n\niii. The trial number is a sequential index (0, 1, 2, ...) within a session. The stored `trial number` variable in the NWB file was not used because it did not agree with the `trial_start` variable.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number*?\n\ni. No processing beyond assigning the loop index. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ninput_curr[2] = trial\n```\n\niii. The trial number is simply the sequential index of the trial within the session.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Previous trial outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `Reward` behavior time series. Reward event timestamps are matched to behavior timestamps, and a binary `isreward` array marks timepoints where rewards occurred.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\nreward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:]\nreward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\nisreward = np.zeros(T)\nisreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n```\n\niii. The `Reward` time series has its own timestamps (not aligned to the behavior sampling rate), so `searchsorted` is used to find the nearest timepoint. An assertion checks that the alignment error is within half a time bin.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. For each trial, check whether any reward event occurred in the *previous* trial's time range. For the first trial, set to 0. The value is constant across all timepoints within a trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nif trial == 0:\n    input_curr[3,:] = 0\nelse:\n    prev_reward_idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial-1], trial_end_idx[trial-1], dtype=int)\n    isprevreward = np.any(isreward[prev_reward_idx])\n    input_curr[3,:] = int(isprevreward)\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify previous trial outcome as binary (omitted=0, rewarded=1). The code checks if any reward event fell within the previous trial's time boundaries.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone location for the current trial. The reward zone boundaries come from the `reward_zone` behavior time series. This variable is non-zero when the mouse is in the reward zone. I combine this with the position of the mouse when this is non-zero to figure out the closest reward zone. To allow for some noise (of unknown source) and unrewarded tasks, I use the Viterbi algorithm to assign zones so that the rewarded positions match the known reward zones and it is uncommon to switch reward zone. \n\nii. Assigning reward zone location for each trial: \n- Before converting, the `survey()` function was used to try to understand all the data values, including whatever is going on with the `reward_zone` variable. It first computes the position of the min and max position of the mouse when `reward_zone` is non-zero:\n```python\nbehavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \ninfo['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\nposition = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\nfor trial in range(ntrials):\n    idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n    pos_trial = position[idx]\n    reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n    if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n        continue\n    info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n```\nThe function `segment_reward_positions()` uses the Viterbi algorithm to assign reward zone labels so that the initial rewarded positions are close to the corresponding rewarded zone:\n```python\nreward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n                    'B': [200, 250],\n                    'C': [320, 370]}\n...\nsigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\nzone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\ndist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n                    startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\ndist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\nlog_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n```\nIt also encourages the reward zone label to not change often:\n```python\npstay = .9\nlog_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\nnp.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n```\n\niii. The paper defines reward zones A, B, C at specific position ranges. These same ranges could also be found, with some translation, in the code (required mapping X -> A, etc.). I plotted the position of the mouse when `reward_zone` was positive (note that the value was between 0 and 6 I think) to determine that the Viterbi approach would likely work, and that the start of the rewarded position was the least noisy variable to use. The class fractions were approximately 1/3 after this, so it seemed correct. \n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. For each timepoint, compute the signed distance from the animal's position to the nearest edge of the reward zone. Distance is 0 when inside the zone, negative when before the zone, and positive when past it.  The continuous distance is then discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0]\n    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1]\n    return distance_to_reward_zone\n\n```\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\n\niii. This matches the paper's concept of distance relative to the reward zone. The reward zone boundaries are from `reward_zone_dict`, inspired by the reference code `Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py`. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. The continuous distance is discretized into 7 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, inf]`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndistance_to_reward_zone_bins = [-np.inf, -50, -10, 0, 1e-6, 10, 50, np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[0] = np.digitize(distance_to_reward_zone, distance_to_reward_zone_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the instructions. The `1e-6` boundary separates exactly 0 (in the zone boundary) from slightly positive. The `-1` corrects `np.digitize` to be 0-indexed.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The position data and neural data share the same time indices within each trial, so no additional alignment is needed.\n\nii.\n```python\nidx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\nspike_data_curr = spike_data[idx]\nposition_curr = position[idx]\n```\n\niii. The `position` variable directly records the animal's position in the VR corridor in cm.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. No processing beyond extracting the per-trial slice and discretizing. Discretized into 5 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, 90, 180, 270, 360, inf]`, corresponding to 90 cm wide bins spanning the 450 cm track. Within the converted trials the position ranges from -0.8 to 451.8 cm, so the open first and last bins absorb the few samples that fall just outside the track (185 of 1,307,665). \n\nii.\n```python\nposition_curr = position[idx]\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\n```python\nposition_bins = [-np.inf,90,180,270,360,np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\n\niii. The raw position values are used directly. Each lap starts at 0 cm and the track is 450 cm long (Methods), so five 90 cm bins over 0 to 450 are equal-sized by construction. The survey's wider -50 to 450 range comes from including the inter-trial teleport period, which is not part of any converted trial.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 5 bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges `[-inf, 90, 180, 270, 360, inf]`, corresponding to 90 cm wide bins spanning the 450 cm track. \n\nii.\n```python\nposition_bins = [-np.inf,90,180,270,360,np.inf]\n...\noutput_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n```\n\niii. The first and last edges are left open so that samples marginally outside the track fall in the end bins rather than in a class of their own.\n\n## 8-d. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases are handled:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: If the number of neural time bins differs from behavior timestamps, data is cropped to the minimum of the two with a warning printed.\n- **Short trials**: Trials with fewer than 50 timepoints (`min_ntimepoints`) are skipped with a warning.\n- **Missing reward zone data**: Trials where `reward_zone` is never active (all zeros) get NaN positions, and the Viterbi algorithm handles these via uniform emission (assignment driven by neighboring trials' transitions).\n- **Reward timestamp alignment**: An assertion verifies reward times align within half a time bin of behavior timestamps.\n\nii.\n```python\nif T != len(timestamps):\n    print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n    T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n    spike_data = spike_data[:T]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n...\nif idx.sum() < min_ntimepoints:\n    print(f\"Skipping trial {trial}...\")\n    istrial[trial] = False\n    continue\n```\n\niii. These are defensive checks found during data exploration. The neural/behavior mismatch occurs in a small number of sessions.\n\n## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are:\n1. **Loading NWB files** (`pynwb.NWBHDF5IO` and reading large arrays) — I/O bound\n2. **The survey step** — loads every NWB file to gather statistics and reward zone positions\n3. **Full conversion** — loads every NWB file again and extracts all trial data\n4. **Saving the .pkl file**\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The NWB files are large (contain full neural recordings). Conversion is otherwise quite fast\n\n## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The per-trial loop in `convert_session_data` (lines 267-332) iterates over each trial sequentially. Some operations like `np.digitize` and `compute_distance_to_reward_zone` could theoretically be applied to the full session arrays before splitting into trials, but the variable trial lengths make this awkward. The Viterbi inner loop (lines 503-507) iterates over states, which could be vectorized with matrix operations.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. The per-trial loop is the natural structure given variable-length trials. Vectorizing would require padding or masking.\n\n## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The survey step and the conversion step both load every NWB file and read the same data arrays (neural, behavior). The survey reads neural data to count neurons and check rates, then the conversion reads it again. The `reward_zone_positions` are computed in the survey and then used in the conversion, but other data (position, speed, lick, etc.) is read twice. \n\nii. N/A\n\niii. I don't think these could be run at the same time, as it would require too much RAM. The Viterbi output created during surveying is required to process the data. It was convenient to run the survey before deciding how to handle the conversion of the data. \n\n## 13-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. 20 <= median_pos <= end + 20:\n            return label\n    return None\n\n\ndef determine_reward_zone_per_trial(pos, rzone, tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n    \"\"\"Determine the active reward zone for each trial.\n\n    For trials where the animal enters the reward zone (rzone > 0),\n    determine which zone from position. For omission trials or trials\n    where the zone is not entered, inherit from neighboring trials.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(tstart_inds)\n    zone_labels = [None] * n_trials\n\n    # First pass: determine from rzone activation\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s, e = tstart_inds[i], teleport_inds[i]\n        rz_mask = rzone[s:e] > 0\n        if np.any(rz_mask):\n            rz_positions = pos[s:e][rz_mask]\n            zone_labels[i] = get_reward_zone_label(rz_positions)\n\n    # Second pass: fill in missing labels from neighbors\n    # Forward fill first\n    last_label = None\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        if zone_labels[i] is not None:\n            last_label = zone_labels[i]\n        elif last_label is not None:\n            zone_labels[i] = last_label\n\n    # Backward fill for any remaining\n    last_label = None\n    for i in range(n_trials - 1, -1, -1):\n        if zone_labels[i] is not None:\n            last_label = zone_labels[i]\n        elif last_label is not None:\n            zone_labels[i] = last_label\n\n    return zone_labels\n\n\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    \"\"\"Compute signed distance from position to nearest edge of reward zone.\n\n    Negative = before zone, 0 = inside zone, positive = after zone.\n    \"\"\"\n    distance = np.zeros_like(position, dtype=float)\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n    distance[before] = position[before] - rz_start\n    distance[inside] = 0\n    distance[after] = position[after] - rz_end\n    return distance\n\n\ndef discretize_distance(distance):\n    \"\"\"Discretize distance to reward zone into 7 bins.\n\n    0: < -50 cm\n    1: -50 to -10 cm\n    2: -10 to < 0 cm\n    3: 0 cm (in reward zone)\n    4: > 0 to +10 cm\n    5: +10 to +50 cm\n    6: > +50 cm\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.zeros_like(distance, dtype=int)\n    bins[distance < -50] = 0\n    bins[(distance >= -50) & (distance < -10)] = 1\n    bins[(distance >= -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n    bins[distance == 0] = 3\n    bins[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n    bins[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n    bins[distance > 50] = 6\n    return bins\n\n\ndef discretize_position(position, n_bins=5):\n    \"\"\"Discretize absolute position into n_bins equal-sized bins.\n\n    Track is 0-450 cm, so bins are 90 cm each.\n    \"\"\"\n    bin_size = TRACK_LENGTH / n_bins\n    bins = np.clip(np.floor(position / bin_size).astype(int), 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return bins\n\n\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"Discretize speed into 5 bins.\n\n    0: < 2 cm/s\n    1: 2-10 cm/s\n    2: 10-20 cm/s\n    3: 20-40 cm/s\n    4: > 40 cm/s\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.zeros_like(speed, dtype=int)\n    bins[speed < 2] = 0\n    bins[(speed >= 2) & (speed < 10)] = 1\n    bins[(speed >= 10) & (speed < 20)] = 2\n    bins[(speed >= 20) & (speed < 40)] = 3\n    bins[speed >= 40] = 4\n    return bins\n\n\ndef correct_lick_sensor_error(licks, tstart_inds, teleport_inds, correction_thr=0.35):\n    \"\"\"Detect and remove trials with erroneous lick detection.\n\n    From behavior.py: trials where >correction_thr fraction of samples\n    have cumulative lick count > 2 are set to NaN.\n    This corresponds to the paper's >30% threshold with cumulative lick > 2\n    (sustained lick rate >= 20 Hz).\n    \"\"\"\n    licks_corrected = np.copy(licks)\n    error_trials = []\n    for i, (s, e) in enumerate(zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds)):\n        trial_licks = licks_corrected[s:e]\n        if len(trial_licks) > 0:\n            frac_high = np.sum(trial_licks > 2) / len(trial_licks)\n            if frac_high > correction_thr:\n                licks_corrected[s:e] = 0  # Set to 0 instead of NaN for binary\n                error_trials.append(i)\n    return licks_corrected, error_trials\n\n\ndef compute_dff_simple(fluorescence, neuropil, tstart_inds, teleport_inds,\n                       neu_coef=0.7, baseline_window=300):\n    \"\"\"Compute simplified dF/F for interneuron detection.\n\n    Following the reference code's approach:\n    1. Neuropil subtraction: F_corrected = F - 0.7 * F_neuropil\n    2. Maximin baseline per trial with 20s window (300 frames at 15 Hz)\n    3. dF/F = (F_corrected - baseline) / |baseline|\n    \"\"\"\n    n_cells = fluorescence.shape[1]\n    n_timepoints = fluorescence.shape[0]\n\n    # Neuropil correction\n    f_corrected = fluorescence - neu_coef * neuropil\n    # Add back neuropil mean per trial to avoid dividing by small numbers\n\n    dff = np.full((n_timepoints, n_cells), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n\n    for s, e in zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n        if e <= s:\n            continue\n        trial_f = f_corrected[s:e, :]\n        # Add back neuropil mean\n        trial_f = trial_f + neu_coef * np.nanmean(neuropil[s:e, :], axis=0, keepdims=True)\n\n        # Smooth\n        trial_smooth = ndimage.uniform_filter1d(trial_f, size=15, axis=0)\n\n        # Maximin baseline: minimum then maximum filter with 20s window\n        win = min(baseline_window, trial_f.shape[0])\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(trial_smooth, size=win, axis=0)\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, size=win, axis=0)\n\n        # dF/F\n        abs_baseline = np.abs(baseline)\n        abs_baseline[abs_baseline < 1e-6] = 1e-6\n        dff[s:e, :] = (trial_f - baseline) / abs_baseline\n\n    return dff\n\n\ndef detect_interneurons(dff, speed, tstart_inds, teleport_inds, threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"Detect putative interneurons by correlation between dF/F and speed.\n\n    From the paper: Pearson correlation > 0.5 between dF/F and running speed.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_cells = dff.shape[1]\n    is_interneuron = np.zeros(n_cells, dtype=bool)\n\n    # Create mask for valid (within-trial) timepoints\n    valid_mask = np.zeros(dff.shape[0], dtype=bool)\n    for s, e in zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n        valid_mask[s:e] = True\n\n    valid_mask &= ~np.isnan(dff[:, 0])\n\n    if np.sum(valid_mask) < 10:\n        return is_interneuron\n\n    speed_valid = speed[valid_mask]\n\n    for cell in range(n_cells):\n        dff_valid = dff[valid_mask, cell]\n        if np.std(dff_valid) < 1e-10 or np.std(speed_valid) < 1e-10:\n            continue\n        r, _ = stats.pearsonr(dff_valid, speed_valid)\n        if r > threshold:\n            is_interneuron[cell] = True\n\n    return is_interneuron\n\n\ndef determine_reward_outcome(reward_ts, timestamps, tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n    \"\"\"Determine if reward was delivered on each trial.\n\n    Match reward timestamps to trial time windows.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(tstart_inds)\n    outcomes = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        t_start = timestamps[tstart_inds[i]]\n        t_end = timestamps[teleport_inds[i]]\n        n_rewards = np.sum((reward_ts >= t_start) & (reward_ts <= t_end))\n        outcomes[i] = 1 if n_rewards > 0 else 0\n\n    return outcomes\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Main conversion\n# ============================================================\n\ndef convert_session(nwb_path, subject_id, session_num):\n    \"\"\"Convert a single NWB session to the target format.\n\n    Returns trial-level data or None if session should be skipped.\n    \"\"\"\n    with h5py.File(nwb_path, 'r') as f:\n        # Extract imaging rate\n        imaging_rate = f['acquisition/TwoPhotonSeries/imaging_plane/imaging_rate'][()]\n\n        # ---- Behavioral data ----\n        pos = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data'][:]\n        speed = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/speed/data'][:]\n        lick = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/lick/data'][:]\n        rzone = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/reward_zone/data'][:]\n        env = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data'][:]\n        trial_start = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/trial_start/data'][:]\n        teleport = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/teleport/data'][:]\n        timestamps = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/timestamps'][:]\n\n        # Reward events (sparse)\n        reward_data = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward/data'][:]\n        reward_ts = f['processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/Reward/timestamps'][:]\n\n        # ---- Neural data ----\n        iscell = f['processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell'][:]\n        planeIdx = f['processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/planeIdx'][:]\n\n        # Check for multi-plane\n        deconv_keys = list(f['processing/ophys/Deconvolved'].keys())\n        has_multi_plane = 'plane1' in deconv_keys\n\n        # Load deconvolved data and fluorescence\n        deconv_p0 = f['processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data'][:]\n        fluor_p0 = f['processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane0/data'][:]\n        neuro_p0 = f['processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane0/data'][:]\n\n        if has_multi_plane:\n            deconv_p1 = f['processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane1/data'][:]\n            fluor_p1 = f['processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane1/data'][:]\n            neuro_p1 = f['processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane1/data'][:]\n\n    # ---- Combine multi-plane neural data ----\n    curated_mask = iscell[:, 0] == 1\n    n_total_cells = iscell.shape[0]\n\n    if has_multi_plane:\n        n_p0 = deconv_p0.shape[1]\n        n_p1 = deconv_p1.shape[1]\n        assert n_p0 + n_p1 == n_total_cells, \\\n            f\"Cell count mismatch: {n_p0}+{n_p1} != {n_total_cells}\"\n        deconv_all = np.concatenate([deconv_p0, deconv_p1], axis=1)\n        fluor_all = np.concatenate([fluor_p0, fluor_p1], axis=1)\n        neuro_all = np.concatenate([neuro_p0, neuro_p1], axis=1)\n    else:\n        deconv_all = deconv_p0\n        fluor_all = fluor_p0\n        neuro_all = neuro_p0\n\n    # ---- Align lengths ----\n    # Truncate all data to minimum length (occasional off-by-one in NWB)\n    min_len = min(len(pos), deconv_all.shape[0])\n    pos = pos[:min_len]\n    speed = speed[:min_len]\n    lick = lick[:min_len]\n    rzone = rzone[:min_len]\n    env = env[:min_len]\n    trial_start = trial_start[:min_len]\n    teleport = teleport[:min_len]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:min_len]\n    deconv_all = deconv_all[:min_len]\n    fluor_all = fluor_all[:min_len]\n    neuro_all = neuro_all[:min_len]\n\n    # ---- Trial boundaries ----\n    tstart_inds = np.where(trial_start > 0)[0]\n    teleport_inds = np.where(teleport > 0)[0]\n\n    # Ensure matching number of starts and teleports\n    n_trials = min(len(tstart_inds), len(teleport_inds))\n    tstart_inds = tstart_inds[:n_trials]\n    teleport_inds = teleport_inds[:n_trials]\n\n    # Remove trials where teleport comes before or at trial start\n    valid = teleport_inds > tstart_inds\n    tstart_inds = tstart_inds[valid]\n    teleport_inds = teleport_inds[valid]\n    n_trials = len(tstart_inds)\n\n    if n_trials < 2:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {subject_id} ses-{session_num}: only {n_trials} valid trials\")\n        return None\n\n    # ---- Interneuron detection ----\n    # Compute dF/F for interneuron detection\n    dff = compute_dff_simple(fluor_all, neuro_all, tstart_inds, teleport_inds)\n    is_interneuron = detect_interneurons(dff, speed, tstart_inds, teleport_inds,\n                                          threshold=INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THRESHOLD)\n\n    # Apply cell filtering: curated AND not interneuron\n    cell_mask = curated_mask & ~is_interneuron\n    n_curated = int(curated_mask.sum())\n    n_interneurons = int((curated_mask & is_interneuron).sum())\n    n_kept = int(cell_mask.sum())\n\n    if n_kept < 2:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {subject_id} ses-{session_num}: only {n_kept} cells after filtering\")\n        return None\n\n    # Filter neural data\n    deconv_filtered = deconv_all[:, cell_mask]\n\n    # ---- Lick sensor error correction ----\n    lick_corrected, error_trials = correct_lick_sensor_error(\n        lick, tstart_inds, teleport_inds, correction_thr=LICK_CORRECTION_THR)\n    # Convert licks to binary\n    lick_binary = (lick_corrected > 0).astype(float)\n\n    # ---- Reward zone per trial ----\n    zone_labels = determine_reward_zone_per_trial(pos, rzone, tstart_inds, teleport_inds)\n\n    # ---- Reward outcomes ----\n    reward_outcomes = determine_reward_outcome(reward_ts, timestamps, tstart_inds, teleport_inds)\n\n    # ---- Environment type per trial ----\n    env_per_trial = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s, e = tstart_inds[i], teleport_inds[i]\n        env_vals = env[s:e]\n        env_valid = env_vals[env_vals >= 0]\n        if len(env_valid) > 0:\n            env_per_trial[i] = int(np.median(env_valid))\n        else:\n            env_per_trial[i] = 0\n\n    # ---- Build trial-level data ----\n    neural_trials = []\n    input_trials = []\n    output_trials = []\n\n    frame_time = 1.0 / imaging_rate  # seconds per frame\n\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s, e = tstart_inds[i], teleport_inds[i]\n        n_timepoints = e - s\n\n        if n_timepoints < 2:\n            continue\n\n        # Neural: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n        neural = deconv_filtered[s:e, :].T.astype(np.float32)\n\n        # Position and speed for this trial\n        trial_pos = pos[s:e]\n        trial_speed = speed[s:e]\n        trial_lick = lick_binary[s:e]\n\n        # ---- Inputs ----\n        # 1. Time from start of trial (seconds)\n        time_from_start = np.arange(n_timepoints) * frame_time\n\n        # 2. Environment type (binary, per trial) - broadcast to match format\n        env_type = float(env_per_trial[i])\n\n        # 3. Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n        trial_number = float(i + 1)\n\n        # 4. Previous trial outcome (binary, per trial)\n        if i == 0:\n            prev_outcome = 0.0  # No previous trial\n        else:\n            prev_outcome = float(reward_outcomes[i - 1])\n\n        # Input array: (4, n_timepoints) for time-varying, or (4,) for per-trial\n        # Time from start is time-varying, others are per-trial\n        # Use (4, n_timepoints) with broadcast for consistency\n        input_data = np.zeros((4, n_timepoints), dtype=np.float32)\n        input_data[0, :] = time_from_start\n        input_data[1, :] = env_type\n        input_data[2, :] = trial_number\n        input_data[3, :] = prev_outcome\n\n        # ---- Outputs ----\n        # Get reward zone for this trial\n        rz_label = zone_labels[i]\n        if rz_label is None:\n            rz_label = 'A'  # fallback\n        rz_start, rz_end = REWARD_ZONES[rz_label]\n\n        # 1. Distance to reward zone (discretized, time-varying)\n        dist = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(trial_pos, rz_start, rz_end)\n        dist_disc = discretize_distance(dist)\n\n        # 2. Absolute position (discretized into 5 bins, time-varying)\n        # Clip position to valid range\n        pos_clipped = np.clip(trial_pos, 0, TRACK_LENGTH)\n        pos_disc = discretize_position(pos_clipped, n_bins=5)\n\n        # 3. Speed (discretized, time-varying)\n        speed_disc = discretize_speed(np.abs(trial_speed))\n\n        # 4. Lick (binary, time-varying)\n        lick_disc = trial_lick.astype(int)\n\n        # 5. Reward zone location (per-trial): 0=A, 1=B, 2=C\n        rz_loc = {'A': 0, 'B': 1, 'C': 2}[rz_label]\n\n        # 6. Reward outcome (per-trial): 0=no, 1=yes\n        rew_out = int(reward_outcomes[i])\n\n        # Output: (6, n_timepoints) for time-varying, per-trial values broadcast\n        output_data = np.zeros((6, n_timepoints), dtype=np.int64)\n        output_data[0, :] = dist_disc\n        output_data[1, :] = pos_disc\n        output_data[2, :] = speed_disc\n        output_data[3, :] = lick_disc\n        output_data[4, :] = rz_loc\n        output_data[5, :] = rew_out\n\n        neural_trials.append(neural)\n        input_trials.append(input_data)\n        output_trials.append(output_data)\n\n    if len(neural_trials) < 2:\n        print(f\"  Skipping {subject_id} ses-{session_num}: only {len(neural_trials)} valid trials after filtering\")\n        return None\n\n    return {\n        'neural': neural_trials,\n        'input': input_trials,\n        'output': output_trials,\n        'n_curated': n_curated,\n        'n_interneurons': n_interneurons,\n        'n_kept': n_kept,\n        'n_trials': len(neural_trials),\n        'imaging_rate': imaging_rate,\n        'zone_labels': zone_labels,\n        'reward_outcomes': reward_outcomes,\n        'env_per_trial': env_per_trial,\n        'lick_error_trials': error_trials,\n    }\n\n\ndef convert_all(data_dir, sample_only=False, max_sessions_per_subject=None):\n    \"\"\"Convert all NWB files to the target format.\"\"\"\n\n    # Discover subjects\n    subjects_dirs = sorted([d for d in os.listdir(data_dir)\n                           if d.startswith('sub-') and os.path.isdir(os.path.join(data_dir, d))])\n\n    subjects = [d.replace('sub-', '') for d in subjects_dirs]\n    print(f\"Found {len(subjects)} subjects: {subjects}\")\n\n    all_neural = []\n    all_input = []\n    all_output = []\n    subject_idx_list = []\n    brain_region_idx_list = []\n\n    total_sessions = 0\n    total_trials = 0\n    total_cells_kept = 0\n    total_interneurons = 0\n\n    for subj_i, (subj_dir, subj_id) in enumerate(zip(subjects_dirs, subjects)):\n        subj_path = os.path.join(data_dir, subj_dir)\n        nwb_files = sorted(glob(os.path.join(subj_path, '*.nwb')))\n\n        if max_sessions_per_subject:\n            nwb_files = nwb_files[:max_sessions_per_subject]\n\n        print(f\"\\nProcessing {subj_id} ({len(nwb_files)} sessions)...\")\n\n        for nwb_file in nwb_files:\n            fname = os.path.basename(nwb_file)\n            session_num = fname.split('ses-')[1].split('_')[0]\n\n            print(f\"  Session {session_num}...\", end=\" \")\n\n            result = convert_session(nwb_file, subj_id, session_num)\n\n            if result is None:\n                continue\n\n            all_neural.append(result['neural'])\n            all_input.append(result['input'])\n            all_output.append(result['output'])\n            subject_idx_list.append(subj_i)\n            # All cells are from CA1\n            brain_region_idx_list.append(np.zeros(result['n_kept'], dtype=int))\n\n            total_sessions += 1\n            total_trials += result['n_trials']\n            total_cells_kept += result['n_kept']\n            total_interneurons += result['n_interneurons']\n\n            print(f\"OK: {result['n_trials']} trials, {result['n_kept']} cells \"\n                  f\"({result['n_curated']} curated, {result['n_interneurons']} interneurons removed, \"\n                  f\"{len(result['lick_error_trials'])} lick error trials)\")\n\n    print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n    print(f\"Total: {total_sessions} sessions, {total_trials} trials, \"\n          f\"mean {total_cells_kept/total_sessions:.0f} cells/session\")\n    print(f\"Total interneurons removed: {total_interneurons}\")\n\n    # ---- Assemble final data dictionary ----\n    data = {\n        'neural': all_neural,\n        'input': all_input,\n        'output': all_output,\n\n        'subjects': subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array(subject_idx_list, dtype=int),\n\n        'brain_regions': ['CA1'],\n        'brain_region_idx': brain_region_idx_list,\n\n        'input_names': [\n            'time_from_trial_start',\n            'environment_type',\n            'trial_number',\n            'previous_trial_outcome',\n        ],\n\n        'output_names': [\n            'distance_to_reward_zone',\n            'absolute_position',\n            'speed',\n            'lick',\n            'reward_zone_location',\n            'reward_outcome',\n        ],\n\n        'output_values': [\n            ['< -50cm', '-50 to -10cm', '-10 to 0cm', '0cm (in zone)', '0 to +10cm', '+10 to +50cm', '> +50cm'],\n            ['0-90cm', '90-180cm', '180-270cm', '270-360cm', '360-450cm'],\n            ['< 2cm/s', '2-10cm/s', '10-20cm/s', '20-40cm/s', '> 40cm/s'],\n            ['no_lick', 'lick'],\n            ['zone_A', 'zone_B', 'zone_C'],\n            ['no_reward', 'reward'],\n        ],\n\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description': 'Virtual reality navigation with hidden reward zones in hippocampal CA1 imaging. '\n                               'Mice navigate a 450cm linear track with one of three possible hidden reward zones '\n                               '(A: 80-130cm, B: 200-250cm, C: 320-370cm). Reward zone location switches across days.',\n            'time_bin_size': 1000.0 / 15.5078125,  # ms (1/imaging_rate * 1000)\n            'temporal_alignment_event': 'start of trial (re-entry into virtual environment at position 0)',\n            'off_start': 0.0,\n            'off_end': None,  # Variable trial length\n            'track_length_cm': 450,\n            'reward_zones': REWARD_ZONES,\n            'n_subjects': len(subjects),\n            'n_sessions': total_sessions,\n            'n_trials_total': total_trials,\n            'imaging_rate_hz': 15.5078125,\n            'neural_data_type': 'deconvolved calcium activity (OASIS algorithm)',\n            'cell_filtering': 'Manual curation (Suite2P) + putative interneuron exclusion (dF/F-speed correlation > 0.5)',\n            'paper': 'Sosa, Plitt & Giocomo (2025) Nature Neuroscience',\n        }\n    }\n\n    return data\n\n\ndef create_sample(data, max_sessions=5, max_trials=20):\n    \"\"\"Create a smaller sample dataset for quick testing.\"\"\"\n    import copy\n    sample = copy.deepcopy(data)\n\n    # Limit sessions\n    n_sessions = min(max_sessions, len(data['neural']))\n\n    sample['neural'] = []\n    sample['input'] = []\n    sample['output'] = []\n    sample['brain_region_idx'] = []\n    subject_idx = []\n\n    for i in range(n_sessions):\n        n_trials = min(max_trials, len(data['neural'][i]))\n        sample['neural'].append(data['neural'][i][:n_trials])\n        sample['input'].append(data['input'][i][:n_trials])\n        sample['output'].append(data['output'][i][:n_trials])\n        sample['brain_region_idx'].append(data['brain_region_idx'][i])\n        subject_idx.append(data['subject_idx'][i])\n\n    sample['subject_idx'] = np.array(subject_idx, dtype=int)\n\n    return sample\n\n\ndef print_sanity_checks(data):\n    \"\"\"Print sanity checks comparing our data to the paper's statistics.\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"SANITY CHECKS\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    n_sessions = len(data['neural'])\n    n_subjects = len(data['subjects'])\n\n    # Paper says: 11 switch mice, 14 days each (but m11 starts day 3 = 12 sessions)\n    print(f\"\\n1. Number of subjects: {n_subjects} (paper: 11 switch mice)\")\n\n    # Sessions per subject\n    for subj in range(n_subjects):\n        n_sess = np.sum(data['subject_idx'] == subj)\n        print(f\"   {data['subjects'][subj]}: {n_sess} sessions\")\n\n    # Paper says: mean 80.5 +/- 7.4 trials per session\n    trials_per_session = [len(data['neural'][i]) for i in range(n_sessions)]\n    print(f\"\\n2. (2025) \"A flexible hippocampal population code\nfor experience relative to reward\" into the standardized decoder format.\n\nProcessing follows the reference paper and code:\n- Neural data: deconvolved calcium activity (OASIS algorithm, already in NWB)\n- Cell filtering: manual curation (iscell) + putative interneuron exclusion (dF/F-speed corr > 0.5)\n- Trial structure: trial_start to teleport boundaries\n- Temporal alignment: start of each trial\n- Reward zones: A=[80,130], B=[200,250], C=[320,370] cm\n- Environment encoding: 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2\n- Imaging rate: ~15.5 Hz per plane\n\"\"\"\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport h5py\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport sys\nfrom glob import glob\nfrom scipy import ndimage, stats\n\n# ============================================================\n# Configuration\n# ============================================================\n\nDATA_DIR = '/app/data'\nOUTPUT_FILE = '/app/converted_data.pkl'\nSAMPLE_OUTPUT_FILE = '/app/sample_data.pkl'\n\n# Reward zone definitions (from behavior.py in reference code)\nREWARD_ZONES = {\n    'A': [80, 130],   # Zone X in code\n    'B': [200, 250],  # Zone Y in code\n    'C': [320, 370],  # Zone Z in code\n}\n\nTRACK_LENGTH = 450  # cm\n\n# Interneuron exclusion threshold (from methods)\nINTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THRESHOLD = 0.5\n\n# Lick sensor error threshold (from behavior.py: correction_thr=0.35)\nLICK_CORRECTION_THR = 0.35\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Helper functions\n# ============================================================\n\ndef get_reward_zone_label(position_when_in_rzone):\n    \"\"\"Determine reward zone label from positions where rzone > 0.\"\"\"\n    if len(position_when_in_rzone) == 0:\n        return None\n    median_pos = np.median(position_when_in_rzone)\n    for label, (start, end) in REWARD_ZONES.items():\n        if start - 20 <= median_pos <= end + 20:\n            return label\n    return None\n\n\ndef determine_reward_zone_per_trial(pos, rzone, tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n    \"\"\"Determine the active reward zone for each trial.\n\n    For trials where the animal enters the reward zone (rzone > 0),\n    determine which zone from position. For omission trials or trials\n    where the zone is not entered, inherit from neighboring trials.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(tstart_inds)\n    zone_labels = [None] * n_trials\n\n    # First pass: determine from rzone activation\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s, e = tstart_inds[i], teleport_inds[i]\n        rz_mask = rzone[s:e] > 0\n        if np.any(rz_mask):\n            rz_positions = pos[s:e][rz_mask]\n            zone_labels[i] = get_reward_zone_label(rz_positions)\n\n    # Second pass: fill in missing labels from neighbors\n    # Forward fill first\n    last_label = None\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        if zone_labels[i] is not None:\n            last_label = zone_labels[i]\n        elif last_label is not None:\n            zone_labels[i] = last_label\n\n    # Backward fill for any remaining\n    last_label = None\n    for i in range(n_trials - 1, -1, -1):\n        if zone_labels[i] is not None:\n            last_label = zone_labels[i]\n        elif last_label is not None:\n            zone_labels[i] = last_label\n\n    return zone_labels\n\n\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    \"\"\"Compute signed distance from position to nearest edge of reward zone.\n\n    Negative = before zone, 0 = inside zone, positive = after zone.\n    \"\"\"\n    distance = np.zeros_like(position, dtype=float)\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n    distance[before] = position[before] - rz_start\n    distance[inside] = 0\n    distance[after] = position[after] - rz_end\n    return distance\n\n\ndef discretize_distance(distance):\n    \"\"\"Discretize distance to reward zone into 7 bins.\n\n    0: < -50 cm\n    1: -50 to -10 cm\n    2: -10 to < 0 cm\n    3: 0 cm (in reward zone)\n    4: > 0 to +10 cm\n    5: +10 to +50 cm\n    6: > +50 cm\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.zeros_like(distance, dtype=int)\n    bins[distance < -50] = 0\n    bins[(distance >= -50) & (distance < -10)] = 1\n    bins[(distance >= -10) & (distance < 0)] = 2\n    bins[distance == 0] = 3\n    bins[(distance > 0) & (distance <= 10)] = 4\n    bins[(distance > 10) & (distance <= 50)] = 5\n    bins[distance > 50] = 6\n    return bins\n\n\ndef discretize_position(position, n_bins=5):\n    \"\"\"Discretize absolute position into n_bins equal-sized bins.\n\n    Track is 0-450 cm, so bins are 90 cm each.\n    \"\"\"\n    bin_size = TRACK_LENGTH / n_bins\n    bins = np.clip(np.floor(position / bin_size).astype(int), 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return bins\n\n\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"Discretize speed into 5 bins.\n\n    0: < 2 cm/s\n    1: 2-10 cm/s\n    2: 10-20 cm/s\n    3: 20-40 cm/s\n    4: > 40 cm/s\n    \"\"\"\n    bins = np.zeros_like(speed, dtype=int)\n    bins[speed < 2] = 0\n    bins[(speed >= 2) & (speed < 10)] = 1\n    bins[(speed >= 10) & (speed < 20)] = 2\n    bins[(speed >= 20) & (speed < 40)] = 3\n    bins[speed >= 40] = 4\n    return bins\n\n\ndef correct_lick_sensor_error(licks, tstart_inds, teleport_inds, correction_thr=0.35):\n    \"\"\"Detect and remove trials with erroneous lick detection.\n\n    From behavior.py: trials where >correction_thr fraction of samples\n    have cumulative lick count > 2 are set to NaN.\n    This corresponds to the paper's >30% threshold with cumulative lick > 2\n    (sustained lick rate >= 20 Hz).\n    \"\"\"\n    licks_corrected = np.copy(licks)\n    error_trials = []\n    for i, (s, e) in enumerate(zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds)):\n        trial_licks = licks_corrected[s:e]\n        if len(trial_licks) > 0:\n            frac_high = np.sum(trial_licks > 2) / len(trial_licks)\n            if frac_high > correction_thr:\n                licks_corrected[s:e] = 0  # Set to 0 instead of NaN for binary\n                error_trials.append(i)\n    return licks_corrected, error_trials\n\n\ndef compute_dff_simple(fluorescence, neuropil, tstart_inds, teleport_inds,\n                       neu_coef=0.7, baseline_window=300):\n    \"\"\"Compute simplified dF/F for interneuron detection.\n\n    Following the reference code's approach:\n    1. Neuropil subtraction: F_corrected = F - 0.7 * F_neuropil\n    2. Maximin baseline per trial with 20s window (300 frames at 15 Hz)\n    3. dF/F = (F_corrected - baseline) / |baseline|\n    \"\"\"\n    n_cells = fluorescence.shape[1]\n    n_timepoints = fluorescence.shape[0]\n\n    # Neuropil correction\n    f_corrected = fluorescence - neu_coef * neuropil\n    # Add back neuropil mean per trial to avoid dividing by small numbers\n\n    dff = np.full((n_timepoints, n_cells), np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n\n    for s, e in zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n        if e <= s:\n            continue\n        trial_f = f_corrected[s:e, :]\n        # Add back neuropil mean\n        trial_f = trial_f + neu_coef * np.nanmean(neuropil[s:e, :], axis=0, keepdims=True)\n\n        # Smooth\n        trial_smooth = ndimage.uniform_filter1d(trial_f, size=15, axis=0)\n\n        # Maximin baseline: minimum then maximum filter with 20s window\n        win = min(baseline_window, trial_f.shape[0])\n        baseline = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(trial_smooth, size=win, axis=0)\n        baseline = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(baseline, size=win, axis=0)\n\n        # dF/F\n        abs_baseline = np.abs(baseline)\n        abs_baseline[abs_baseline < 1e-6] = 1e-6\n        dff[s:e, :] = (trial_f - baseline) / abs_baseline\n\n    return dff\n\n\ndef detect_interneurons(dff, speed, tstart_inds, teleport_inds, threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"Detect putative interneurons by correlation between dF/F and speed.\n\n    From the paper: Pearson correlation > 0.5 between dF/F and running speed.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_cells = dff.shape[1]\n    is_interneuron = np.zeros(n_cells, dtype=bool)\n\n    # Create mask for valid (within-trial) timepoints\n    valid_mask = np.zeros(dff.shape[0], dtype=bool)\n    for s, e in zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n        valid_mask[s:e] = True\n\n    valid_mask &= ~np.isnan(dff[:, 0])\n\n    if np.sum(valid_mask) < 10:\n        return is_interneuron\n\n    speed_valid = speed[valid_mask]\n\n    for cell in range(n_cells):\n        dff_valid = dff[valid_mask, cell]\n        if np.std(dff_valid) < 1e-10 or np.std(speed_valid) < 1e-10:\n            continue\n        r, _ = stats.pearsonr(dff_valid, speed_valid)\n        if r > threshold:\n            is_interneuron[cell] = True\n\n    return is_interneuron\n\n\ndef determine_reward_outcome(reward_ts, timestamps, tstart_inds, teleport_inds):\n    \"\"\"Determine if reward was delivered on each trial.\n\n    Match reward timestamps to trial time windows.\n    \"\"\"\n    n_trials = len(tstart_inds)\n    outcomes = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        t_start = timestamps[tstart_inds[i]]\n        t_end = timestamps[teleport_inds[i]]\n        n_rewards = np.sum((reward_ts >= t_start) & (reward_ts <= t_end))\n        outcomes[i] = 1 if n_rewards > 0 else 0\n\n    return outcomes\n\n\n# ============================================================\n# Main conversion\n# ============================================================\n\ndef convert_session(nwb_path, subject_id, session_num):\n    \"\"\"Convert a single NWB session to the target format.\n\n    Returns trial-level data or None if session should be skipped.\n    \"\"\"\n    with h5py.File(nwb_path, 'r') as f:\n        # ---- Neural data ----\n        iscell = f['processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/iscell'][:]\n        planeIdx = f['processing/ophys/ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation/planeIdx'][:]\n\n        # Check for multi-plane\n        deconv_keys = list(f['processing/ophys/Deconvolved'].keys())\n        has_multi_plane = 'plane1' in deconv_keys\n\n        # Load deconvolved data and fluorescence\n        deconv_p0 = f['processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane0/data'][:]\n        fluor_p0 = f['processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane0/data'][:]\n        neuro_p0 = f['processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane0/data'][:]\n\n        if has_multi_plane:\n            deconv_p1 = f['processing/ophys/Deconvolved/plane1/data'][:]\n            fluor_p1 = f['processing/ophys/Fluorescence/plane1/data'][:]\n            neuro_p1 = f['processing/ophys/Neuropil/plane1/data'][:]\n\n    # ---- Combine multi-plane neural data ----\n    curated_mask = iscell[:, 0] == 1\n    n_total_cells = iscell.shape[0]\n\n    if has_multi_plane:\n        n_p0 = deconv_p0.shape[1]\n        n_p1 = deconv_p1.shape[1]\n        assert n_p0 + n_p1 == n_total_cells, \\\n            f\"Cell count mismatch: {n_p0}+{n_p1} != {n_total_cells}\"\n        deconv_all = np.concatenate([deconv_p0, deconv_p1], axis=1)\n        fluor_all = np.concatenate([fluor_p0, fluor_p1], axis=1)\n        neuro_all = np.concatenate([neuro_p0, neuro_p1], axis=1)\n    else:\n        deconv_all = deconv_p0\n        fluor_all = fluor_p0\n        neuro_all = neuro_p0\n\n    # ---- Align lengths ----\n    # Truncate all data to minimum length (occasional off-by-one in NWB)\n    min_len = min(len(pos), deconv_all.shape[0])\n    pos = pos[:min_len]\n    speed = speed[:min_len]\n    lick = lick[:min_len]\n    rzone = rzone[:min_len]\n    env = env[:min_len]\n    trial_start = trial_start[:min_len]\n    teleport = teleport[:min_len]\n    timestamps = timestamps[:min_len]\n    deconv_all = deconv_all[:min_len]\n    fluor_all = fluor_all[:min_len]\n    neuro_all = neuro_all[:min_len]\n\n    # ---- Trial boundaries ----\n    tstart_inds = np.where(trial_start > 0)[0]\n    teleport_inds = np.where(teleport > 0)[0]\n\n    # Ensure matching number of starts and teleports\n    n_trials = min(len(tstart_inds), len(teleport_inds))\n    tstart_inds = tstart_inds[:n_trials]\n    teleport_inds = teleport_inds[:n_trials]\n\n    # ---- Build trial-level data ----\n    neural_trials = []\n    input_trials = []\n    output_trials = []\n\n    frame_time = 1.0 / imaging_rate  # seconds per frame\n\n    for i in range(n_trials):\n        s, e = tstart_inds[i], teleport_inds[i]\n        n_timepoints = e - s\n\n        if n_timepoints < 2:\n            continue\n\n        # ---- Inputs ----\n        # 1. Time from start of trial (seconds)\n        time_from_start = np.arange(n_timepoints) * frame_time\n\n        # 2. Environment type (binary, per trial) - broadcast to match format\n        env_type = float(env_per_trial[i])\n\n        # 3. Trial number (continuous, per trial)\n        trial_number = float(i + 1)\n\n        # 4. Previous trial outcome (binary, per trial)\n        if i == 0:\n            prev_outcome = 0.0  # No previous trial\n        else:\n            prev_outcome = float(reward_outcomes[i - 1])\n\n        # Input array: (4, n_timepoints) for time-varying, or (4,) for per-trial\n        # Time from start is time-varying, others are per-trial\n        # Use (4, n_timepoints) with broadcast for consistency\n        input_data = np.zeros((4, n_timepoints), dtype=np.float32)\n        input_data[0, :] = time_from_start\n        input_data[1, :] = env_type\n        input_data[2, :] = trial_number\n        input_data[3, :] = prev_outcome\n\n        # ---- Outputs ----\n        # Get reward zone for this trial\n        rz_label = zone_labels[i]\n        if rz_label is None:\n            rz_label = 'A'  # fallback\n        rz_start, rz_end = REWARD_ZONES[rz_label]\n\n        # 1.
